#2848. Milwaukee Bucks vs. Toronto Raptors Preview & Game Thread: Process over results

The Milwaukee Bucks nearly beat a Sixers team contending for a championship on Saturday, helped by a marquee 50-point performance from Tyler Herro and a career-high day for Brayden Burries. After the misery of a 13-game losing streak, Milwaukee fans got a glimmer of hope in their three-point win amid an ugly rebuild. The Bucks now travel to Canada with renewed optimism and momentum, facing a Toronto Raptors squad that recently snapped a six-game losing streak and has been underwhelming at the start of the season. The Raptors beat the Bucks 121-107 earlier this month and have won four straight overall against Milwaukee.

The Raptors are struggling to start the season. Analysts expected Toronto to hover around the playoff field and potentially challenge Philadelphia, Boston, and New York for supremacy in the Eastern Conference—but the Raptors have come nowhere close. Despite winning their first two games, they sit tenth in the East after 16 games. That includes a loss to the Celtics, a loss to the Sixers, and two back-to-back losses to the Knicks. Toronto's star acquisition, Kawhi Leonard, has missed the last 14 games with recurring knee issues and seems weeks away from returning.

Without Leonard, Toronto has looked stuck on offense. The Raptors have struggled to assert themselves on the perimeter, allowing opposing defenses to sag off unimpressive shooters like Scottie Barnes and RJ Barrett and pack the paint. Despite a tough, gritty defense that allows just 111.4 PPG, Toronto has looked uninspired in half-court offense, contributing to a mediocre start to the season. They've also struggled with injuries; Barrett will miss some weeks with a hamstring strain. Toronto recently snapped their losing streak with a 105-102 win over Detroit, led by a go-ahead layup from Scottie Barnes. However, they'll need more than that to re-enter the contention conversation.

Ousmane Dieng: Questionable (left finger fracture)

Bogoljub Markovic: Out (G league assignment)

Kawhi Leonard: Out (knee injury management)

Collin Murray-Boyles: Probable (concussion protocol)

Allen Graves has eased into a role for Toronto in Barrett's absence. The 2026 first-rounder has been elite defensively, posting 0.6 steals and 0.4 blocks in 16.1 minutes per game, pestering some of the league's best ballhandlers like Detroit's Cade Cunningham. Graves is also a driven rebounder—he's hauled in 1.9 offensive rebounds per game, again, on limited minutes, as an undersized big. He's also had some big scoring nights, like Friday's win over the Pistons, where he scored 20 points on 6/9 shooting. Graves has emerged as the Raptors' Swiss Army knife and a high-energy, high-impact stretch forward.

Who: Milwaukee Bucks (1-16) vs. Toronto Raptors (6-10).

After his outburst against the 76ers, Tyler Herro scores over 30 points on efficient shooting, and Toronto's backcourt struggles to find an answer for him as the Bucks take their second win of the season.

#2842. Knicks 113, Pacers 106: “Knicks handled their business”

They say a big part of being a good golfer is not how you play when you pump drives 300 yards down the middle of every fairway, see the rough only from a distance, or sink putts from anywhere on the premises. Your quality is rather defined by the afternoons when you do not have your A game in the bag. When you spray it left and right, when you have to grind around the greens. Karl-Anthony Towns apparently has enjoyed golf since childhood, and Josh Hart likes to tee it up a bit as well. And while they might be just like most mere mortals when their swings get a bit shaky, they sure know how to play basketball when they do not have their A game going.

The poor and pummeled Indiana Pacers might argue that KAT was feeling damn fine and fit since he poured a nasty 30/13/5 line over their scalps. Those who understand the Knicks—and a tiny bit of basketball—of course can recognize that it's not normal for the big Meowth to hit the side of the backboard with an open corner jumper or turn into Chris Dudley at the free throw line. Fuming, the Pacers might then argue that Josh Hart always plays like that, bereft of a shot or any kind of functioning shooting motion, yet hell-bent on doing everything else from rebounding to pushing the pace to cleaning up the MSG concession stands. And they'd be wrong here as well. The soul of the team has been hurting lately, both mentally and physically, and was hurting in the early stages of the game, too.

In the end, it doesn't matter what the Pacers think because "the Knicks handled their business" yesterday, as the wonderfully named 90sRback pointed out in the Postgame Thread. They didn't flinch when Julian Slawson turned into a reincarnation of the great Bobby Jones or Jalen Brunson got bumped numerous times by that third-string guard who looks like he could play a cringe-worthy Harfoot in that abomination of a TV show called Rings of Power. They also did not worry when the Pacers answered every quick power surge with a little, ugly run of their own and just could not be shaken off entirely, kind of like a film of sweat on a hot summer's day. The Knicks didn't care about any of that. They just plodded along, hitting mid-iron after mid-iron to the middle of the green. And then sprinkled in a few big monster drives as well.

Most of those came from their big man Karl-Anthony Towns. Sick the night before, he needed a bit of time to get going but then reminded everyone watching in the third quarter why quite a few people consider him one of the most skilled bigs of our generation. Crossovers between the legs, passes that Magic Johnson would be proud of, nasty step-back jumpers, the old Mark Jackson shimmy—KAT had it all going for about ten minutes. The younger Larry Nance probably is still wondering where that behemoth went that he thought he had cornered a few seats away from Spike Lee and how something that big can disappear that quickly. Or glide into the paint like he was Jean Ratelle skating over the blue line, for that matter. People sometimes say that KAT is not Nikola Jokic, and they are right. But man, Nikola Jokic or anybody else isn't KAT either.

Josh Hart might actually even up the Big Bodega in the uniqueness department. It is astonishing how much the 31-year-old does while seemingly lacking most of the skills that modern analytics consider indispensable for an impactful NBA wing. Yeah, Hart is having an impact alright, you guys sitting in front of a computer. Now, it's not always pretty, and we all could do without some of the mental lapses or lazy passes. But in the end, you just cannot argue that the New York Knicks need Josh Hart. Maybe not in as big a role as he has right now, maybe not even as a frontline starter. But they need him. Jalen Brunson definitely knows it, and that gifted dunk he let Hart have on a two-against-none fast break was a clear sign of that.

Maybe he can do the same for Mikal Bridges next game. A day after hitting the most spectacular shot of the young Knicks season so far, he fell back into the hole that he had dragged himself out of painfully slowly over the last few games. He clanked shot after shot, he had people running by his jogging self in transition, and he just looked like he was upset the game took him off his couch on Sunday night. Now, give him credit: he battled Pascal Siakam late in a matchup that you really do not want to see as a New York fan, but all in all, it just is not enough right now from a starter on a team with ambitions.

Pacome Dadiet has those as well, we believe, but that's probably the best you could say about his night. The young guy goes all out and fights hard but frankly doesn't look skilled enough to really have an impact now or in the future. You almost have to feel for him when you see his eyes after a big or clumsy mistake. Deep down inside, he seems to know it himself. In the end, he is just a kid trying to live his dream, but no matter how badly you want it, sometimes you have to realize that things might not work out. Nobody thought it would be something you could even remotely think about with Mo Diawara as early as this season, but the fact that Dadiet is seeing minutes in these games before him should sound the alarm. And last season's biggest surprise should send all the fire trucks he can find to deal with this emergency.

Diawara won't lack for prime examples of how to turn a career around in his very own locker room. Landry Shamet (again), Jose Alvarado (amazing response to losing his primary backup spot and earning it back recently), and Andre Drummond (weird but true) all played like consummate professionals yesterday, impacting a game in which their team needed more from them than they are usually accustomed to giving. That's another hallmark of a good team. Role players step up when they are counted on, guys fight back when they lose their spot in the rotation. They deal with adversity.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2841. Federal Reserve Board announces enforcement action against American Express Company to address, among other things, the firm’s failure to sufficiently detect and report certain suspicious activity related to money laundering

For release at 4:30 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced an enforcement action against American Express Company to address, among other things, the firm's failure to sufficiently detect and report certain suspicious activity related to money laundering.

The Board also identified significant deficiencies in how American Express Company's enterprise-wide anti-money laundering program was implemented, in particular at the firm's subsidiary national bank. The Office of the Comptroller of the Currency, the primary federal regulator of the firm's national bank, has issued a separate enforcement action against the bank.

The Board's action will help ensure that the firm operates in compliance with all U.S. laws and regulations.

#2837. Federal Reserve Board releases results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 10:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Friday released the results of the 2025 Survey of Consumer Finances, which provides the public and policymakers with detailed insights into the economic condition of American families.

The data provide a representative picture of what Americans own, how and how much they borrow, and how they bank. The current version of the survey has been undertaken every three years since 1989 and provides the public with an extensive time series of thorough information on the economic conditions of American families. Alongside the survey data, the Board issued its summary report entitled Changes in U.S. Family Finances from 2022 to 2025 (PDF), which examines changes in income, net worth, assets, debt, and financial vulnerability.

Key findings from the 2025 survey include:

In addition to the summary report, an interactive chartbook displaying changes in these financial characteristics over time is available here. Additional data from the survey, including extensive data examining other economic conditions of American families, is made available to researchers and the public here.

The survey is conducted for the Board by NORC, a social science research organization at the University of Chicago. Participants in the study are chosen at random from 119 geographic areas, including metropolitan areas and rural counties across the United States, using scientific sampling procedures that aim to produce a sample representative of American families.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call (202) 452-2955.

#2832. NBA winners and losers after one month: Wemby soars, injuries pile up

The first four weeks of the NBA season have been nothing but unpredictable.

And one month in, the narratives and storylines have already started to take shape, as teams work to climb up the standings.

We saw old faces like LeBron James (Philadelphia 76ers), Giannis Antetokounmpo (Miami Heat) and Jaylen Brown (76ers) make their impacts on their new homes.

And we even saw a player, San Antonio Spurs phenom Victor Wembanyama, "shunk" a ball, or put it through the net in a hybrid shot-dunk.

Here are the winners and losers from the first month of the 2026-27 NBA regular season.

MORE: Cameron Boozer grabs top spot in NBA rookie rankings as Grizzlies surge

It seems like, every week, he's doing something that hasn't been done in the history of basketball. Now in his fourth season in the NBA, Wembanyama is averaging career bests in points (29.9 per game), rebounds (12.3), blocks (3.9), steals (1.3) and is averaging a career low in turnovers (2.2). The most impressive thing about his offense, however, is the ruthless efficiency with which he's playing.

Wembanyama is no longer chasing forced, low-percentage shots. His attempts are coming basically at the rim or from 3-point range. His defensive presence is singular and is en route to his second consecutive unanimous Defensive Player of the Year award; he was the first to ever win the award unanimously. He is playing with confidence. He's playing his best basketball yet.

Their last three games – wins by a combined six points against the Hornets (4-11) and Kings (2-14) and a loss against the Jazz (3-15) – showed some regression, but Austin Reaves (heel) was out. When he was on the floor, however, the Lakers looked like one of the best teams in the NBA. Now that LeBron James is no longer with the team, Dončić has become the unquestioned engine of the offense. He's dropping historic numbers and has taken complete ownership of the offense.

The Lakers also put complementary pieces around him, players such as Walker Kessler, a lob threat and rim protecting center who's giving Dončić an outlet for easy offense. Once Reaves returns, Los Angeles looks like a team that should keep contending.

Though the competition hasn't been stiff, the Sixers are on a five-game winning streak, Joel Embiid is returning to form and LeBron James is making a significant winning impact. Not only that, but Jaylen Brown and Tyrese Maxey are also generating exceptional scoring numbers. It's safe to expect this team, which is still learning how to play together, to improve even more as the season wears on.

But Philadelphia hasn't really blown out many opponents, and its most recent win, by three over the 1-16 Bucks, nearly went to overtime after Milwaukee nearly erased a 13-point lead in the second half.

You would never have thought that the Timberwolves had lost their best player and the most dynamic two-guard in the NBA just four games into the season. Minnesota is trucking along in the West. The offense – behind LaMelo Ball, Ayo Dosunmu and Jaden McDaniels – is doing just enough. It's the defense, the one that leads the NBA in rating, allowing just 103.8 points per 100 possessions, that's carrying the team.

Once Edwards returns, this could be a team to contend for the Western Conference title.

In Oklahoma City's game Sunday, Nov. 22 against the Heat, only nine players were available. Reigning two-time MVP Shai Gilgeous-Alexander, who suffered a lower back contusion on a hard fall two days earlier, was not one of them. Against Miami, it was Oklahoma City's 16th game of the season; it was the eighth different lineup the Thunder had deployed. It's tough to compete with that kind of disadvantage.

The Golden State Warriors (Stephen Curry), Minnesota Timberwolves (Anthony Edwards), Indiana Pacers (Tyrese Haliburton), Toronto Raptors (Kawhi Leonard), Detroit Pistons (Jalen Duren), Orlando Magic (Paolo Banchero) and Utah Jazz (Jaren Jackson Jr., Lauri Markkanen) are also among the teams that have lost a player with at least one All-Star appearance because of injuries.

The Jazz, after years of irrelevance and tanking, were seemingly in position to take a step forward. That simply hasn't materialized. Utah is turning over the ball far too much, the offense isn't scoring what it needs to and some of the younger players haven't stepped up after vets went down with injuries.

The Bulls lost star rookie Caleb Wilson (fractured right hand) and Norman Powell hasn't been able to carry the team to a consistent level of play. The biggest issue is a defense that ranks 29th in rating (119.3) and simply allows opponents to score far too easily. Neither team looks even close to competing for a playoff spot.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: NBA winners and losers after one month: Wemby soars, injuries pile up

#2828. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

The Federal Open Market Committee approved the following statement for release by a 10 â 2 vote:

The Committee decided to maintain the target range for the federal funds rate at 3-3/4 to 4 percent, in support of the Federal Reserve's dual mandate. The Committee is continuing its policy of maintaining ample reserves in the banking system.

Economic activity is expanding at a strong pace. While uncertainty remains elevated owing, in part, to geopolitical developments, domestic spending has been resilient. Productivity growth is strong, and capital investment is robust. Job gains have kept pace with the workforce, and the unemployment rate has changed little.

Inflation remains elevated. The accumulation of more information will help the Committee determine the policy stance appropriate to ensure a timely return of inflation to the Committee's 2 percent goal. The Committee will deliver price stability.

Voting against the monetary policy action were Lisa D. Cook and Beth M. Hammack, who preferred to raise the target range for the federal funds rate by 1/4 percentage point at this meeting.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

#2824. Fred VanVleet is quietly having his most efficient season for Rockets

Despite the tough loss to the Denver Nuggets on Friday, the Houston Rockets are off to a good start to the season. The Rockets are 12-5, even though they have had one of the toughest schedules to start the season. The Rockets are getting ready for a new week after a weekend off, before starting a four-game homestand on Monday as they welcome the Los Angeles Clippers.

The Rockets, as expected, are led by Alperen Sengun and Kevin Durant, who have built great chemistry over their first two seasons together. Sengun, in particular, is off to a great start as the only player in the league leading his team in points, rebounds, and assists. Sengun is also having the best season of his career from the 3-point range, shooting 50 percent and attempting a career-high two 3-pointers a game.

Durant continues, even two decades into his career, to be one of the best players in the NBA, remaining one of the league's most efficient players even while taking a career-low number of shots per game. Another player, however, whose return is one of the biggest reasons for the Rockets' good start to the season is Fred VanVleet.

When it was announced that VanVleet would be ready for the start of the season, everyone expected him to have a positive impact as a leader on and off the court, but so far he has been much more than a distributor; he is having the most efficient season of his career.

HOUSTON, TX – NOVEMBER 2: Amen Thompson #1 and Fred VanVleet #5 of the Houston Rockets talk during the game against the Boston Celtics on November 2, 2026 at the Toyota Center in Houston, Texas. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and or using this photograph, User is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by Logan Riely/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

When VanVleet first arrived in Houston, the Rockets were a team in transition. They had been through several years of rebuilding, finishing at the bottom of the standings each season as they looked to bring in high draft picks. The plan worked, as they landed multiple top-five picks, but it didn't lead to wins immediately. The Rockets knew it was time to pivot from rebuilding to winning, and that started with bringing in players like Steven Adams, Jeff Green, Dillon Brooks, and, more importantly, VanVleet.

VanVleet came in as a former All-Star and NBA champion, which is exactly what the Rockets needed. The veteran point guard's calming presence paid immediate dividends: the Rockets went on a 19-win turnaround in his first season, the best in the NBA that season.

The Rockets realized how important VanVleet is to the team as he became the Rockets' on- and off-court leader from day one. If anyone outside the organization doubted his importance, last season confirmed it: VanVleet missed the entire season, and the Rockets at times struggled without his guiding hand, especially on offense.

With VanVleet back leading the team, the Rockets have a top-five offense and are much better organized to start the season. However, it isn't just his leadership that has shone this season. VanVleet's offense, especially his efficiency, has been on another level.

In VanVleet's second season, his offensive production dipped. VanVleet had his second-worst 3-point and field-goal percentages of his career in 2024-25, and some people started to question whether he had lost a step on the offensive end of the court.

That notion was amplified as many believed he would struggle again this season, coming off a major leg injury at 32. However, that hasn't been the case, as VanVleet is not only bouncing back offensively; he's also having the most efficient season of his career.

VanVleet has career highs in effective field goal percentage, true shooting percentage, and overall field goal percentage, and his .392 from the 3-point range is the second-best of his career. This is actually more impressive than his career high of .414 in his second season because it is coming on almost four more attempts per game. Probably the most impressive part of his game this season has been his shooting inside the arc, as for the first time in his career, he is shooting over 50 percent from the 2-point range at .517.

The original theory when Durant first arrived in Houston was that players like VanVleet would have an easier time getting good shots with all the attention Durant draws from the defense. That plan was put on hold when VanVleet missed the entire 2025-26 season, but it is bearing fruit this season, as VanVleet has taken full advantage of the double teams Durant and Sengun see every game.

Just having VanVleet back as the leader of the team is a big deal for the Rockets, but he's not only brought his calming presence back; he has also brought the most efficient offense of his career and a big reason for the Rockets' strong start to the season.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2819. 2026-27 NBA Power Rankings Week 6: Spurs stay on top while Heat, Knicks move into top 10

Welcome to the latest edition of the weekly NBC Sports NBA Power Rankings, where the Spurs still look like the best team in the land. With Thanksgiving coming up, we focused on something each team should be thankful for.

Record: 14-3Ranking last week: No. 1Games this week: at Sacramento, at Portland, Lakers, Celtics

Something to be thankful for: Victor Wembanyama's loyalty. As much as San Antonio should be thankful for Wembanyama's MVP-level start to the season — 29.9 points, 12.3 rebounds and 3.9 blocks per game — what matters even more is his Duncanesque loyalty to the Spurs. "I think this is really the ideal environment because even in other cities, where we're not the home team, people aren't as respectful. People can be very persistent or intrusive sometimes, but never here..." Wemby said postgame recently. "I'd say it's the ideal environment to spend 15 or 20 years." One other Wembanyama note: He has 72 dunks this season, which not only leads the league but is more dunks than 20 NBA teams have. He made so many it was hurting his hand, so he decided to jump-shoot it from directly above the rim (which is officially registered as a dunk).

Record: 14-3Ranking last week: No. 3Games this week: Utah, at San Antonio, at Memphis

Something to be thankful for: Lakers role players stepping up. Of course the Lakers needed Luka Doncic to be special (and he's been playing at an MVP level), and they needed Austin Reaves and Walker Kessler to play up to their new contracts. But what really has the Lakers this sharp to start the season is the strong play from the role players. Matisse Thybulle and Ziaire Williams are forcing turnovers and defending well on one end and hitting their 3s on the other. Sandro Mamukelashvili has been fantastic and Quentin Grimes showed how well he was playing, dropping 47 on the Hornets. Lakers coach JJ Redick put it this way after the win over Charlotte: "We're 15 games in. I don't know what this is going to look like after 82 games. I don't know what it's going to look like after 50. But I do know that there's something with this group, and every special group doesn't mean you win a championship, but it's a special group of guys."

Record: 12-5Ranking last week: No. 4Games this week: Brooklyn, at Oklahoma City, Memphis, at Sacramemto

Something to be thankful for: Tim Connelly and the aggressive Minnesota front office. The Timberwolves had a good team, and most GMs would have looked at what they had around Anthony Edwards and tinkered around the edges. Connelly was aggressive and took a big swing on LaMelo Ball being ready for the bigger stage, and so far that has worked out. There were other smart moves, particularly adding Ayo Dosunmu, but also Jonathan Kuminga, Trey Lules and Bones Hyland have had their moments. Connelly also trusted Rudy Gobert to stay the defensive force he has been. When Anthony Edwards returns from getting his knee scoped, this might be the second-best team in the West.

Record: 14-3Ranking last week: No. 6Games this week: Portland, Miami, at Indiana, at Houston

Something to be thankful for: The 76ers' clutch play. Philadelphia is 12-0 in clutch games this season (within five points in the final five minutes). It's a good thing and a testament to the talent on this roster. LeBron James, Tyrese Maxey, Joel Embiid and Jaylen Brown are all making plays, with LeBron seemingly the glue that just finds a way to get things done. It's concerning that the 76ers have only had five non-clutch games and are 2-3 in those, but they'll take the wins and bank them early in the season as things are still coming together in Philadelphia.

Record: 10-5Ranking last week: No. 9Games this week: Dallas, at Utah, at Toronto

Something to be thankful for: Jamal Murray. He is an All-NBA, All-Star and NBA Champion, but is Jamal Murray having his best season? Could be. He's averaging 24.9 points a game, shooting 46.3% from beyond the arc. On a team where Nikola Jokic is Nikola Jokic but many of the role players have been uninspiring and inconsistent, Murray is getting the Nuggets wins with the most efficient shooting season of his career (65 true shooting percentage). Those numbers don't even include his big 45-point night Sunday against the Bulls.

Record: 12-5Ranking last week: No. 5Games this week: LA Clippers, Phoenix, Philadelphia

Something to be thankful for: Fred VanVleet taking care of the ball. Last season, the Rockets were 27th in the league in turnover percentage. This season, they are up to 14th, not elite but a huge step in the right direction. Credit that to having a true point guard in Fred VanVleet in the house. The other stat of note is that the Rockets' elite defense has been pedestrian of late, ranking 21st in the league over their last seven games, and the result is that they are 5-4 in their last nine games.

Record: 12-5Ranking last week: No. 10Games this week: at Charlotte, LA Clippers, at Timberwolves, Lakers

Something to be thankful for: The young front core of Cameron Boozer and Zach Edey, with Cedric Coward and Jaylen Wells on the wing. In 130 minutes together this season, that foursome is rock-solid defensively, giving up less than a point per possession and posting a +25.4 net rating. The soft schedule the Grizzlies have played may have inflated that number somewhat, but watch the group and it's a young core that just clicks. The goal this season was for Memphis' young core to grow and develop, but it looks like it's already become a playoff-caliber team.

Record: 11-6Ranking last week: No. 11Games this week: Cleveland, Miami, Atlanta

Something to be thankful for: Karl-Anthony Towns. While some of the Knicks' role players appear to be nursing a championship hangover at the start of the season, their two stars have picked up where they left off — and Towns is playing better than a season ago. KAT is averaging 21.3 points and 13.9 rebounds a game, and he is playing arguably the best defense in years. Those role players might be starting to come around — which is why the Knicks have won five in a row — but it's Towns and Jalen Brunson who have shown up from Day 1 this season.

Record: 10-6Ranking last week: No. 14Games this week: at Philadelphia, at New York, at Cleveland

Something to be thankful for: Davion Mitchell. Of course the Heat should be thankful Giannis Antetokounmpo chose to come to Miami and instantly made the franchise a threat in the East — and he is putting up MVP-ballot numbers (28.2 points, 12.6 rebounds, and 5.5 assists a game). However, it is the play of point guard Mitchell that has helped bring it all together and spark the Heat's current run. Mitchell, amazingly, leads the NBA in assists and is averaging 17.2 points and nine assists a game, and he dropped six 3-pointers on Milwaukee last week (video below, although that's mostly just an excuse to show off the amazing Vice City court and uniforms.

Record: 11-6Ranking last week: No. 2Games this week: at Washington, Atlanta, at San Antonio

Something to be thankful for: The way the Celtics start games. Boston has a league-best +23.5 net rating in the first quarter. Or, look at it this way: The Celtics outscore opponents by an average of 5.9 points in the first quarter (also a league-best). That falls to outscoring opponents by 4.3 in the second quarter, then they play teams basically even in the second half. Joe Mazzulla should have concerns about those second halves in the big picture, especially in the midst of a four-game losing streak that included ugly road losses to Sacramento and Curry-less Golden State.

Record: 11-6Ranking last week: No. 15Games this week: at Boston, at New York

Something to be thankful for: Kingston Flemings. It took eight games for Hawks coach Quin Snyder to replace the 35-year-old CJ McCollum in the starting lineup with 19-year-old Kingston Flemings, and once that happened, the starting five's pace picked up the and offense has looked better on the eye test (even if the numbers don't back that up in a very small sample size). As a starter, Flemings is averaging 8.6 points and 3.2 assists a game, and his potential to be the point guard of the future is evident. (He's missed the last three games with a back issue, while McCollum is out about a month with a calf strain.)

Record: 10-6Ranking last week: No. 7Games this week: at Orlando, Milwaukee, Orlando

Something to be thankful for: Ausar Thompson's offensive development, especially as a playmaker. The Pistons have one of the game's elite playmakers in Cade Cunningham, but last season's playoffs showed the need for a secondary one to take pressure off their MVP candidate, and the Pistons didn't make an offseason move to add one. Thompson has started to fill that role, and it showed in the win against the Spurs last week when J.B. Bickerstaff put the ball in his hands for stretches and he set up Cunningham and Daniss Jenkins. Thompson also is setting more ball screens than he has in his career, and he's become a clever cutter to the rim. He's not a shooter and likely never will be a great one, but he's figured out how to be a real contributor on offense by playing to his strengths.

Record: 10-7Ranking last week: No. 12Games this week: at Indiana, Minnesota, at Milwaukee

Something to be thankful for: Ajay Mitchell and Jared McCain. In a season where the Thunder have their second, third and fifth-best players out injured, these guys who were supposed to be the punch off the bench have stepped up. Mitchell is averaging 17 points and 3.6 assists a game, while McCain is averaging 13.9 points and is spacing the floor, shooting 34.2% from beyond the arc. Right now, the Thunder are just trying to keep their head above water until they get key players back, and those two — along with Shai Gilgeous-Alexander — are at the heart of that push.

Record: 10-6Ranking last week: No. 8Games this week: at Philadelphia, San Antonio, Golden State, New Orleans

Something to be thankful for: Having Damian Lillard home. This just feels right. Of course Lillard has been great on the court, averaging 20.6 points a game and shooting 34.2% from 3-point range, but having him back in Portland is more than that. It's about setting the organization on the right path. We also could have easily gone with Toumani Camara as the reason for Trail Blazers fans to be thankful, the elite defender has become a much bigger offensive threat, hitting 38% from beyond the arc this season.

Record: 9-8Ranking last week: No. 16Games this week: at Chicago, at New York, Miami

Something to be thankful for: Meleek Thomas. The second-round pick out of Arkansas has been one of the biggest surprises of this impressive rookie class, averaging 12.7 points a game (fourth-most on the team) while shooting 42% from 3-point range. He's also shown flashes of being a defensive playmaker. The Cavaliers are +1.9 per 100 possessions when he is on the court, which is promising for a rookie. The fact that Kenny Atkinson is giving him 20+ minutes a night on a veteran team should tell you all you need to know.

Record: 8-7Ranking last week: No. 18Games this week: at New Orleans, at Houston, Dallas, at Utah

Something to be thankful for: Collin Gillespie. All the rumors around the Suns' trade interest in Dejounte Murray — and all that buzz is coming out of New Orleans and Murray's camp, not Phoenix or the Suns — are based on the idea that the Suns need a point guard. Except the Suns have a quality point guard on the roster already in Gillespie. After a slow start to the season, Gillespie has found a groove of late, and it might be time to move him into the starting lineup ahead of the slumping Jalen Green.

Record: 10-7Ranking last week: No. 17Games this week: Boston, Orlando

Something to be thankful for: Cooper Flagg. Don't overthink this, Cooper Flagg is the foundation of this franchise going forward, and in his second season, he looks like an All-NBA-level player, if not top-10 in the league. And he doesn't even turn 20 until next month. Flagg has taken a big step forward this season, averaging 21.4 points, 6.5 rebounds, and 6.4 assists a game on an efficient 59.3 true shooting percentage, and he's already a high-level defender. The basketball gods smiled on Mavericks fans in their moment of pain after the Luka Doncic trade, and this could not have worked out better.

Record: 7-11Ranking last week: No. 24Games this week: Boston, Orlando

Something to be thankful for: AJ Dybantsa. Let's not overcomplicate this. Anthony Davis has been fantastic to start the season and Trae Young is… playing. However, Dybantsa is averaging 16.5 points and 6.9 rebounds per game, and is already showing he is the future and the guy the Wizards need to build around. They also need to find a coach who can maximize him, and the rumors around the league have Brian Keefe as the coach on the hottest seat so far.

Record: 9-8Ranking last week: No. 13Games this week: Sacramento, at Portland, LA Clippers

Something to be thankful for: Yaxel Lendeborg. There hasn't been a lot to be thankful for the past five games in Golden State, as they have been an offensive mess since Stephen Curry went out (and he's going to be out a few more weeks). Still, the rookie Lendeborg has been a revelation, averaging 19.7 points and 7.3 assists a game, and with Curry out, he's found the ball in his hands as a primary shot creator. Lendeborg is finding life as the top guy on the scouting report a challenge, but the rookie has been everything the Warriors needed this season (and what they hoped Jonathan Kuminga would be). Whatever and whenever the era after Curry steps away becomes, Lendeborg should be part of it.

Record: 7-9Ranking last week: No. 19Games this week: Detroit, at Washington, at Detroit

Something to be thankful for: Franz Wagner, who may be playing the best basketball of his career right now. He's thriving as Orlando's No. 1 option, averaging 25.1 points per game, plus 8.4 assists and 6.8 rebounds a night, while taking on the primary scoring and shot-creation role with Paolo Banchero still out injured. Franz even hit the game-winner to beat his brother Moe and the Brooklyn Nets.

Record: 6-10Ranking last week: No. 20Games this week: Milwaukee, Brooklyn, Denver

Something to be thankful for: Immanuel Quickley. In the Raptors' win over the Pacers this week (snapping a six-game losing streak), Quickley stepped up with his best playmaking game of the season, dishing out 10 assists to go with his 18 points. He had the ball in his hands more and ran the pick-and-roll masterfully, finding open shooters and rolling bigs. Until Kawhi Leonard returns, Toronto needs more of that.

Record: 5-12Ranking last week: No. 21Games this week: Oklahoma City, Philadelphia, at Chicago

Something to be thankful for: Pascal Siakam. This isn't the first time Pascal Siakam has been on a team that didn't have enough support around him, he experienced it before in Toronto. Siakam is averaging 23 points and 6.1 assists a night, with an impressive 60.2 true shooting percentage — he is every bit an All-Star this season. He's had to carry a lot of the offensive load with Tyrese Haliburton out (and Haliburton will remain out for a few more weeks), and that hasn't gone smoothly, with Indiana 3-10 without their star point guard. Siakam has done his best to keep this team afloat, but he needs more help.

Record: 4-12Ranking last week: No. 26Games this week: Cleveland, at Charlotte, Indiana

Something to be thankful for: Jalen Smith. While Caleb Wilson looks like a future star, he's an inconsistent rookie and, for now, he's out (fractured hand). Smith has been the Bulls' best player this season, averaging 13.4 points and a team-high 7.9 rebounds a game, but more importantly, he's just been very efficient, shooting 70.5% on two-pointers and 36.4% from beyond the arc. New Bulls coach Tiago Splitter trusts him, which is more than we can say for Matas Buzelis, who was benched in the fourth quarter of the loss to the Wizards (and that isn't the first time this season, it's concerning because Buzelis is a key part of this team's future).

Record: 4-11Ranking last week: No. 23Games this week: at Houston, at Memphis, New Orleans, at Golden State

Something to be thankful for: The Clippers 3-point shooting. The Clippers aren't taking an extraordinary amount of 3s, their 37.7 attempts per game is 14th in the league, but as a team they are shooting 38.7% from deep. Some of this may not be sustainable: Derrick Jones Jr. is shooting 48.1% (on 3.7 attempts per game), Brook Lopez is at 42.2%, Rui Hachimura is at 40.8%, and Grady Dick is at 48% (but just 1.7 attempts per game). The best news for the Clippers is that Brandon Ingram is set to return to the court Monday night, they need his offense.

Record: 3-12Ranking last week: No. 22Games this week: at Minnesota, at Milwaukee, at Toronto, at Charlotte

Something to be thankful for: Day'Ron Sharpe. There's not a lot to be thankful for in Brooklyn to start the season, but Sharpe — the longest-tenured Net — has stepped up as the starting center this season, and while he's not putting up massive counting stats (9 points and 7.4 rebounds a game (3.5 of those rebounds offensive), the struggling Nets are a net positive when he is on the court this season. He's long been a favorite of the analytics crowd, but given a larger role this year, he's showing he's ready for it.

Record: 5-13Ranking last week: No. 27Games this week: Phoenix, at LA Clippers, at Portland

Something to be thankful for: Jeremiah Fears. I have no idea what the future of the Pelicans will look like, but whatever it is, Fears has to be a big part of it. The second-year point guard has taken on a larger role this season and is averaging 26.7 points, 5.3 assists and 4.9 rebounds a game. Even when his shot is not falling, he keeps attacking, and Pelicans fans have to love that mindset.

Record: 4-11Ranking last week: No. 25Games this week: Memphis, Chicago, Brooklyn

Something to be thankful for: Christian Anderson Jr. As ugly as things have been for the Hornets lately, there are positives. Watching them against the Lakers reminded me that Naz Reid is just a reliable, solid basketball player. However, the Hornets are about the long term and finding young players who can be part of the future, and Christian Anderson looks like someone who fits that mold. In his last five games he's averaging 9.4 points and shooting 44% from beyond the arc. "Just getting more comfortable in what positions I need to be in, getting more confident, and being ready to attack and be aggressive, whether it's scoring the ball or facilitating," Anderson said last week.

Record: 3-15Ranking last week: No. 29Games this week: at Los Angeles Lakers, Denver, Phoenix

Something to be thankful for: Darryn Peterson. While it seems a bit on the nose to be thankful for the No. 2 pick, he has started to figure it out lately. He scored 20 points with 10 rebounds and eight assists against the Suns on Friday, and before the game Devin Booker had nothing but praise for the rookie: "He's definitely [someone] I see a lot of potential in. He already plays like he's been in the league a couple of years. Just the maturity in his game. The pace to his game. He's got a real bright future ahead of him." Peterson is a huge Booker fan and has said he modeled his game after him, so postgame Booker gave Peterson a signed jersey.

Record: 2-14Ranking last week: No. 28Games this week: San Antonio, at Golden State, Minnesota

Something to be thankful for: Darius Acuff Jr. Of course there are the stats: 25.4 points per game, and now, after dropping 36 on the Suns last week, he has five 30+ point games in 15 career games. There is legitimate concern about his efficiency numbers, which need to improve, but one thing that stands out is that when he struggles early, he pushes through, he doesn't defer or go into a shell — shooters shoot, and Acuff is that. It's a good long-term sign. Also, the Kings needed the home win and to light the beam, like they did Saturday night thanks to Keegan Murray's tip-in.

Record: 1-16Ranking last week: No. 30Games this week: at Toronto, Brooklyn, at Detroit, Oklahoma City

Something to be thankful for: Tyler Herro breaking out, and Jaime Jaquez Jr. It's difficult to find the positives in Milwaukee, but there are some. Jaquez has been a good player for most of his young career, but this is the first season he's knocking down 3-pointers at this rate (42.5%) while averaging 14.6 points a night. Herro has been up and down since returning to Wisconsin, but he may be finding his stride, and he dropped a career-best 50 on the 76ers this week.

#2818. Federal Reserve Board issues enforcement action with former employee of United Community Bank

#2814. Federal Reserve Board announces pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, and changes to streamline and adjust their check services

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 1:30 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced pricing, effective January 1, 2027, for payment services the Federal Reserve Banks provide to banks and credit unions, such as the clearing of checks, automated clearing house (ACH) transactions, instant payments, and wholesale payment and settlement services.

By law, the Federal Reserve must establish fees to recover the costs, including imputed costs, of providing payment services over the long run. The Federal Reserve expects to recover 101.5 percent of actual and imputed expenses in 2027, including the return on equity that would have been earned if a private-sector firm provided the services. Overall, price changes for 2027 will result in an estimated 1.4 percent average price increase for established, mature services.

In addition, the Federal Reserve Banks announced changes to streamline and adjust their check services. As described in the request for information on check services issued last year, the volume of checks processed by the Federal Reserve Banks is declining, but checks remain an important payment instrument for many consumers and businesses. The changes were informed by public feedback and ensure that the Federal Reserve Banks continue to provide check services efficiently. The adjustments include optimizing check processing windows during low-volume periods, increasing fees that banks and credit unions pay to process checks, and other operational updates. These changes do not impact consumers' and businesses' ability to pay with or receive checks and only impact how banks and credit unions process checks with Federal Reserve Banks.

The entire 2027 fee schedule for all of the Federal Reserve's payment services is published on FRBservices.org®. Additional information on the Federal Reserve's payments services, including service usage statistics, is available on the Board's website.

#2813. Dwight Howard regrets shutting down the Magic's attempt to trade for Steph Curry

Dwight Howard regrets shutting down the Magic's attempt to trade for Steph Curry originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

In an alternate timeline, Steph Curry is representing the Orlando Magic.

Curry has established himself as an NBA legend over the past 18 years, but his tenure in the Bay Area could have been over soon after it began.

On "The AM Hoops Show," former Magic superstar Dwight Howard told host Casey Keirnan that he shot down an opportunity to team up with the future two-time MVP in Orlando.

"At the time, I wanted to play with Monta Ellis," Howard said. "The Magic wanted to get Steph, and I said 'No.' I said no because I didn't know. I didn't know Wardell Steph Curry was gonna be one of the greatest players in the world."

When asked to expand, Howard clarified that Curry was dealing with injuries during the 2011-12 NBA season.

"(Steph) was hurt … At the time, Monta was the one who was playing all the minutes," Howard said. "Stupid. No, I regret it."

Howard proceeded to go silent for several seconds while appearing to think about his decision to shut down the trade.

At the time, Howard was a three-time Defensive Player of the Year in the midst of a stretch of five consecutive First-Team All-NBA selections.

Howard averaged 20.6 points and 13.9 rebounds per game during that span, leading the NBA in rebounds four times and blocks twice, while taking the Magic to the 2009 NBA Finals.

Curry, meanwhile, dealt with recurring ankle issues that threatened to derail his young NBA career. He played just 26 games in the lockout-shortened 2011-2012 season, as Golden State went 23-43.

Orlando traded Howard to the Los Angeles Lakers the following offseason, starting Howard's decline from superstardom.

Howard later tweeted that he had the chance to play with Curry during the 2022-23 season but wasn't signed by Warriors general manager Mike Dunleavy Jr.

Curry has become one of the greatest players in league history, and it is interesting to see how one trade rejection changed the course of the NBA.

#2812. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2810. Micah Parsons still needs clearance to play on Wednesday

Green Bay Packers head coach Matt LaFleur isn't being very forward about the team's injury situation leading up to their Thanksgiving Eve matchup against the Los Angeles Rams. File it under gamesmanship.

In the post-practice media availability in the locker room, though, edge defender Micah Parsons told the press that he still has one more hurdle he needs to jump before he's allowed to play against the Rams this week: Final clearance from surgeon Dr. Neal ElAttrache.

ElAttrache, who did Parsons' knee surgery, is widely considered the top knee expert for NFL players in the post-Dr. James Andrews era. According to Parsons, he will have a conference call with ElAttrache after media availability.

Parsons has been able to participate in all of the Packers' practices this week without a knee brace. He also stated, according to The Athletic's Matt Schneidman, that friends and family of his have bought tickets to the games on Wednesday.

Things seem to be lining up for a Parsons return, but there's still no official word on his status. The Packers won't need to make a corresponding roster move for Parsons' addition to the active roster, as the team released running back Pierre Strong Jr. (and later re-signed him to the practice squad) earlier this week. Presumably, that open roster spot will end up going to Parsons whenever he's activated.

Update: Rams reporter Adam Grosbard of the Los Angeles Daily News is reporting that Rams edge defender Myles Garrett stated that Parsons requested extra tickets from Garrett for Wednesday's game.

#2809. Agencies extend comment period for proposed third-party risk management guidance

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:15 p.m. EST Share

The Federal Deposit Insurance Corporation, the Federal Reserve Board, the National Credit Union Administration, and the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency today announced that they will extend, until December 16, the comment period for proposed guidance on managing risks associated with third-party relationships. Comments on the proposal were originally due November 16.

The proposed guidance reflects the agencies' supervisory experience and lessons learned from examining financial institutions' third-party risk management practices. It is intended to help banks and credit unions tailor their risk management practices to the risks associated with individual third-party relationships. The proposed guidance takes a principles-based approach and, like all supervisory guidance, is non-binding.

When finalized, the federal bank regulatory agencies plan to rescind existing third-party risk management guidance and replace it with the finalized guidance to promote consistency and prudent innovation in the banking industry. NCUA also plans to rescind its existing third-party risk management guidance and will conduct its own reviews to update related guidance as needed.

Also today, the Federal Reserve Board separately announced that it will extend, until December 16, the comment period on a proposed third-party risk management guide specifically for Federal Reserve-supervised traditional community banks.

Federal Register notice: Proposed Third-Party Risk Management Guide for Traditional Community Banking Organizations

#2807. Hawks’ second unit stars in comeback victory over Pistons

The Atlanta Hawks rallied in the fourth quarter to dispatch the Detroit Pistons 107-97 at State Farm Arena on Sunday afternoon to win their fourth consecutive game. Nickeil Alexander-Walker led the Hawks with 27 points while Corey Kispert added 18 points off the bench. For the Pistons, Isaiah Joe scored 19 points while former Hawk John Collins added 18 points.

Heading into the game, it was unclear whether Pistons star Cade Cunningham would suit up, and while he would start this game it didn't take long for the Hawks to draw him into foul trouble as the Hawks started this game on the front foot behind Jalen Johnson's eight first quarter points as the hosts got themselves out on the run often in the first half, scoring 18 of their 23 fastbreak points in the first half.

The Hawks led for the majority of the first half behind strong bench play (with Dyson Daniels leading the bench unit), with Kispert in particular looking lively with 10 points. After the Hawks took a double-digit lead in the second quarter, the Pistons went on a 16-4 run to take their first lead of the game.

While a quiet first half from Alexander-Walker (eight points) quickly burst into life in the third quarter, the Pistons tightened the screw defensively, and behind Collins and Ausar Thompson (combining for 18 points in the third) began applying pressure on the Hawks offensively — Collins from deep, Thompson on his aggressive drives.

With the Pistons finding their groove, they established a double-digit lead of their own, and an 11-point lead heading into the fourth quarter. It seemed unlikely at the time that the Hawks were going to be able to pull a result here, with the Pistons defensively clicking, and with Cunningham emerging into the game as he struggled with foul trouble and injury. …And that's when it happened.

Behind a lineup of Daniels, Aaron Wiggins, Kispert, Lu Dort, and Zuby Ejiofor, the Hawks began the fourth quarter and contributed to an 18-0 run — holding the Pistons scoreless in the opening seven minutes of the fourth quarter. Here's some of the plays that were pretty important for the Hawks coming back, many of them led by Lu Dort.

Off of a Detroit turnover, Kispert splits the double team, finds Ejiofor, who finds Dort at the baseline and muscles his way to an 'and-1' opportunity:

Off of a good find from Daniels to Wiggins, Dort collects the offensive rebound and finds Kispert for a three-pointer to bring the Hawks within one:

Dort would then connect on a three to give the Hawks a lead, his high-arcing three connecting off of the find from Kispert:

Defensively, the Hawks were forcing turnovers, Cunningham was settling into jumpshots, and Kevin Huerter was unable to get his shots to fall in this run. The Hawks were shifting defensively, and Kispert draws a charge on this possession on Thompson's drive:

The Pistons finally scored their first points of the fourth quarter with 4:52 remaining, off of which Hawks head coach Quin Snyder called an immediate timeout and re-inserted Alexander-Walker, Onyeka Okongwu, and Johnson. Alexander-Walker took over, with Dort also continuing his strong fourth quarter, and the Hawks would re-take a double-digit lead after a dagger three from Alexander-Walker put the Hawks up by 12 points with just over a minute remaining. While the Hawks held the Pistons to single-digits for much of the quarter, in the end, the Hawks outscored Detroit 33-12 in the final frame, overturning a double-digit deficit heading into the fourth quarter after a strong third quarter from the visitors.

"Rather than try to compare and contrast, I think you just look at that group of five guys that were out there just competing," began Snyder when asked about the difference between the third and fourth quarter. "Corey, Wiggs, and Lu, Zuby, obviously — I'd be remiss if I left out Dyson — but that five-man line-up, just the level of tenacity, defensively was unique. You could feel it in the way they competed on the offensive boards. They took threes and they crashed, and tried to rebound and defend, and that's what we were trying to accomplish, trying to do, and they actually went out there and did it."

How did the Hawks' bench achieve what Snyder and the Hawks wanted them to accomplish?

"Focus," said Snyder on how the second unit remained connected. "Commitment to that, to each other. I think it begins with our spacing coming down the floor. If we don't have something, passing the ball. Where we're attacking the defense, not on the front side where they're shifted, not holding the ball where the defense can shift again, and being willing to create shots for each other, and taking them, and crashing (the offensive glass). It's kind of formulaic. Those guys, when you're coming into the game, you have that focus. Particularly when your team's behind, there's an opportunity there. Those guys have done that and seized it."

Whether the Hawks have a lead or are trailing, the bench's approach is the same, according to Corey Kispert, who led the bench scoring with 18 points.

"That's just kind of the mindset that we have going into each game," said Kispert of the bench connection. "Either we turn the game around or we carry what the starters have already started. Either way, that doesn't affect what we do. We try to come in and change the game and then hand it back. Nights like tonight really help us carry that on, carry that forward. We're a super connected group, especially on that bench."

While the margin of bench scoring was narrowly in the Hawks' favor (47-43), their production was still a significant contributor to winning this game and I think their plus/minus tells a more accurate and fitting story as to their contribution to this game. The lowest plus/minus from a bench player was Aaron Wiggins' plus-18 (!!) — that's the lowest from the bench unit, and considering he shot 4-of-11 from the field and 1-of-8 from three, that's probably fair.

In the absence of Jock Landale, Zuby Ejiofor continues to see rotation minutes and, again, was impactful with his energy. He got an opportunity to post a few more shots, shooting 2-of-9 from the field and missing four three-pointers, but as a screener and as a threat on the offensive/defensive glass he was certainly effective, once again. As briefly highlighted, Lu Dort was excellent, particularly in the fourth quarter where he scored 10 of his 16 points, playing the entirety of the fourth quarter. Offensively, Dort was crashing the offensive glass, buying extra possessions, and defensively was a large factor on Cade Cunningham's difficult evening shooting the ball. Dort was fantastic in the initial comeback to erase the Pistons' lead and chipped in along with Alexander-Walker to finish the job.

Finally, there's the play of Corey Kispert. Kispert has been a DNP-CD when the Hawks were fully healthy, but with an injury to CJ McCollum, Kispert has seen an increase in opportunities and he is taking them — a season-high 18 points on 7-of-13 from the field and 4-of-10 from three.

Snyder was full of praise for not just Kispert's level of play but also his mental fortitude and the energy he brings off the bench.

"It's hard to overstate it, and it's something I've had direct conversations about," said Snyder about Kispert. "He's played well, even up to the point where he's got to get on the floor to have more minutes. You want to play everybody, and the game doesn't always present itself that way, but we are — and I've said this before too — a work in progress. The thing that's most impressive about what Corey's done is obviously his play, but his mental toughness and his commitment to the team has been unwavering through all that. Keep throwing yourself in the team, and that isn't something I even need to say, but in my mind, the way that he's been able to lose himself in the group is pretty unique. It's really unique, and that's one of the reasons that I think he's been so ready to impact and to play. His minutes certainly have been impactful, but I think his energy, as much as anything, you can feed off that. He's doing that in every situation. You guys have covered enough basketball. It doesn't happen. It's pretty unique, he's a pretty unique player."

What's been most impressive about Kispert is not just his ability to shoot the ball (48% from three so far this season) but his ability to open up his game by putting the ball on the floor and driving to the rim.

"That is the skill," said Kispert on his shooting and driving. "That's what has made me an NBA player. You don't either shoot it or drive it, and obviously shooting it is my main thing, that's my main skill, that's my NBA-level skill, but it's really easy to guard somebody that can't drive as well. So, being able to read whether to drive it, whether to shoot it, or pass it and move it, that's the skill that I've gotten to get really good at over the course of my career to stick around. When you can do both and finish the rim and also shoot threes at a really high level, you become really, really hard to guard."

Kispert was especially a threat when the Hawks grabbed offensive rebounds; 24 of them to be exact, as the Pistons are still without Jalen Duren and forced to play smaller. When the Hawks snatched second chances, Kispert was never far away.

"There's points when we've just been so focused collectively and sometimes that focus drifts," said Snyder on the defensive effort. "Over the course of a 48-minute game, rarely do you get it for 48 — especially when you're playing a team as good as Detroit — where you have it and they score. Good defense, better offense. I think if that connectivity that we have on defense, being disciplined, crushing the ball, but then also in help situations. One of the key things for us is to finish possessions, to rebound. You don't want those efforts on certain possessions to go wasted. We've talked about that being a big part of our identity. The challenge for us is when we don't get to play defense, meaning if we take a tough early shot after not making a pass, or we turn the ball over early, it's really hard to defend in those situations. You're not going to be perfect on offense, but that's something for us to keep focusing on so we get a chance to play defense."

"The way that you get threes against a really good defensive team like Detroit is offensive rebounds," said Kispert. "It's really hard for them to track the ball, track it off the rim, and then go find me, Aaron, whoever to go guard. That's where you need to get your most open looks. I don't know, it's something about the ball coming to you from the basket, it makes it easier to shoot if it's like practice versus having to catch it coming sideways off of a pick and roll, or off the dribble, or anything like that. It feels more like practice when you get it off of an offensive rebound and shoot. Statistically, those are the ones that go on the most. those are the highest value threes that we can get. You definitely want to hunt them and shoot them to get those chances."

The Hawks scored 27 second chance points, and having been on the other side where teams will grab offensive rebounds and score, the Hawks know exactly how deflating it can be and were acutely aware of that fact as they ran riot on the glass.

"It was the most important," said Kispert of the Hawks' rebounding edge. "Lu tore it up, Zuby tore it up, Dyson tore it up on the glass. Plays like that allows more chances to score and deflates the other team. We talk about that all of time as a team … when it's rebound after rebound after rebound and a big three, that flattens the other team."

The key, according to Snyder, is committing to the offensive glass even if you may not come up with the rebound as it may lead to a tip, or another avenue to keep the ball alive. This is something Dyson Daniels has had a particular nose for recently, grabbing 14 rebounds in this game, including seven offensive rebounds.

"I think we have to commit to going," said Snyder of the offensive rebounding. "Even if we don't have somebody that individually is—there's guys in this league that are getting four or five a game. I think we have to send numbers to the glass. Dyson's been pretty unusual, he's been able to do the rebound the ball. He can also keep the balls alive. I think that's part of it, we have to see it as a collective effort. Even if you don't get it, you may tip it. We may get it off the floor because someone has to account for somebody in the glass. The transition defense is much easier to play when you're offensive rebounding, particularly if there's less transition opportunities. Again, that's why it's so important for us to take our shots because we get a chance to do that as opposed to defending against something more difficult. It's just something that we know we have to do. Our guys committing to that the way they have is just really important to our efficiency on offense."

Daniels' fingerprints were all over last night's game. While he scored just five points on 2-of-5 shooting, he came up with 14 rebounds, seven offensive rebounds, eight assists, five steals, and two blocks in a game-high 38 minutes. Even with the return of Kingston Flemings to the lineup, Daniels is an instrumental element of the Hawks' playmaking, and this was even more so the case as, incredibly, Jalen Johnson registered zero assists in this game, struggling to make an impact beyond the first quarter.

Flemings' return to the lineup was a welcome one, and he produced two highlight plays upon his return. The first in which John Collins drops to the floor (tripping over Flemings' foot) and sinks the jumpshot:

The second is this two-handed dunk at the rim on the drive:

A fantastic turn of pace to shift gears, and to finish with authority; a very welcome return for Kingston Flemings.

"He was really the guy that kind of turned the pace for us towards the end of the third quarter," said Daniels of Flemings. "He was able to get downhill, got a few buckets, create some opportunities for us and change the momentum of the game … he did a really good job tonight."

The star of the second half was Alexander-Walker, who scored 27 points on 8-of-18 shooting from the field and 6-of-10 from three — 19 of those points and five of those threes coming in the second half alone. It's an odd one, because the boxscore would indicate that Alexander-Walker dominated proceedings here, but his impact on this game was understated, and I mean that positively. Yes, he had a that initial third quarter blitz, but he didn't jack up shot after shot as can happen in other games, allowing the game to come to him and taking over from the bench unit excellently in the fourth quarter to finish what they started.

Between the execution of the bench and the comeback nature of a game that felt as though it was slipping away in the second half, this is arguably the Hawks' highest quality victory of the season, and one that lifts them to second in the Eastern Conference.

This week will be an interesting week indeed, as the Hawks (11-6) will look to win a fifth straight game as they take on the Boston Celtics (11-6, and have lost four straight games) in a defining NBA Cup game for Group C. The Celtics will be in action on Tuesday in Washington in the NBA Cup, while the Hawks will have a chance to recover. After the Celtics, comes a test against the New York Knicks in what will be an entertaining weekend!
