#2653. What Alijah Vera-Tucker returning to practice means for the Patriots

The New England Patriots returned to work on Monday, officially ending their bye week. When they did, they saw a member of their starting offensive line make his comeback: left guard Alijah Vera-Tucker participated in practice for the first time since suffering a hamstring injury in Week 6.

Vera-Tucker's return means that he has now officially been designated to return from injured reserve and is eligible to rejoin New England's active roster as soon as this week's game against the Los Angeles Chargers. Let's find out how this impacts the Patriots.

The actual on-field impact of Vera-Tucker returning to the 53-man roster will be discussed once he is actually activated. For now, however, let's focus on the return designation itself and it's impact on player and team.

First of all, the timeline of his return. At the moment, Vera-Tucker remains on injured reserve albeit with the "designated for return" attachment next to his status. This signifies that his window to come back has now opened and will remain so for the next 21 days. He can be activated at any point during those three weeks, and if not would revert to regular IR for the remainder of the season.

Obviously, with return designations limited — more on those in a second — the expectation is that the 27-year-old will return to the 53-man roster during that 21-day period. That activation might happen as early as Friday, when the Patriots are set to fly to L.A. for their game against the Chargers.

The Patriots currently have 53 players on their active roster, the maximum allowed by the NFL during the regular season. This means that whenever the team decides to actually activate Vera-Tucker, a follow-up move will become necessary.

What that move will look like is anybody's guess at this point in time. It could be as simple as cutting a player from the roster, possibly from an offensive line that would be 11 players deep once Vera-Tucker is fully back.

Vera-Tucker has extensive starting experience at both left and right guard, but the belief is that he will return to his previous position in the Patriots lineup when all is said and done. While this should help solidify the left guard spot — he looked solid in his first six games of the season — the right guard position is where the real intrigue now starts.

With Mike Onwenu sidelined and increasingly unlikely to return at any point this year, the Patriots realistically have three starting options available: Greg Van Roten, Ben Brown and Matt Pryor. Van Roten was the next man up at right guard after Onwenu broke his ankle in Week 2; Brown took over for him when he went to IR one game later; Pryor has started the last four games.

Having Vera-Tucker back allows the Patriots to use their interior O-line resources differently, and the process of doing so is already getting started this week.

Every NFL team is allowed to bring up to eight players back from injured reserve during the regular season, and Vera-Tucker has now taken the sixth of those spots.

Previously, the Patriots designated wide receivers A.J. Brown and Mack Hollins, fullback Reggie Gilliam, and offensive linemen Marcus Bryan and Van Roten for activation. All of them have since made their way to the 53-man roster, but even if they hadn't their return spots would still count against the eight available.

This also means that the Patriots have only two spots remaining to be used on a group of players consisting of RB TreVeyon Henderson, WR Romeo Doubs, RG Mike Onwenu, ED Quintayvious Hutchins, LB Khalil Jacobs and S Dell Pettus. QB Drake Maye, who is expected to miss at least six weeks after fracturing his fibula is not even part of that group; he remains on the active roster for now.

His future is uncertain. What seems like a safe bet, on the other hand, is that the seventh returnee will be Romeo Doubs.

#2652. Fantasy Basketball Top 200 Rankings: How concerned should Trae Young managers be?

With the Washington Wizards boasting a revamped rotation headlined by first-overall pick AJ Dybantsa and veterans Anthony Davis and Trae Young, there were expectations that the team would take a step forward in 2026-27. While the team's current record of 7-11 doesn't scream out "progress," it is an improvement compared to how the Wizards started last season. But among the concerns has been Young's play, as he is going through one of the most inefficient shooting stretches of his NBA career.

Through 18 games, Young is averaging 17.1 points, 2.8 rebounds, 8.8 assists, 1.3 steals and 2.1 three-pointers per game. The assists, steals and three-pointers have been fine, but the points and percentages have taken significant hits. Young finished Sunday with shooting splits of 36.4 percent from the field and 32.5 percent from beyond the arc. If it held, the former would be the lowest of Young's career by a significant margin, and he hasn't shot this poorly from three since his rookie season (32.4 percent).

Despite Young's continued struggles, Washington went 3-1 last week, including a blowout win over the Magic on November 22. In those four games, Young averaged 12.8 points, 2.3 rebounds, 9.0 assists, 1.8 steals and 2.5 three-pointers per game, shooting 28.6 percent from the field.

He was better from three, making 40 percent of his attempts, and Young has kept his turnover count in check on most nights. Washington's improved rotation should be acknowledged, as it allows Young to take a step back as a scorer. But given his second-round ADP, fantasy managers expected a bigger return than what they've seen so far.

#2651. Shane Steichen says Colts have not shut down Alec Pierce for the season

A report from NFL Media on Sunday morning indicated that the Colts have decided to pull the plug on hopes that wide receiver Alec Pierce will return to action this season, but it appears to have been premature.

Pierce has not played since aggravating a heel injury that required offseason surgery in Week 2 and the Colts’ recent roster moves have made it clear that they have shifted their attention to the 2027 season. While that may be the case, head coach Shane Steichen said on Monday that the team has not made any call about whether Pierce will return in their final six games.

“I know there’s reports out there on Alec Pierce being done for the season,” Steichen said, via Stephen Holder of ESPN.com. “We have not made that determination. He’s still going through his rehab process. The only way we’ll put him out there is if he’s 100 percent. We gotta be really smart.”

Pierce has a short window to get to 100 percent before the season is over and bringing him back from a long layoff may not be the best way to ensure that he’s at full strength for next season, so it may just be a matter of when rather than if the Colts ultimately decide to shut the wideout down until next year.

#2650. Packers vs. Rams bold predictions for NFL Week 12

Packers vs. Rams bold predictions for NFL Week 12 appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

The Green Bay Packers and Los Angeles Rams will play a rare Wednesday night game before Thanksgiving, giving NFL fans a matchup between two teams looking to strengthen their playoff positioning.

The game will be streamed on Netflix, with both teams entering the week at 6-4. The Rams will be without Puka Nacua and Davante Adams, while the Packers could get Micah Parsons back for his first game since tearing his ACL.

Here are three bold predictions for Wednesday night's matchup.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Love will find the end zone with his legs against the Rams.

Whether it comes on a quarterback sneak, a designed run near the goal line, or an escape from the pocket, I expect Love to score a rushing touchdown on Wednesday night.

The Rams have played well defensively, particularly in the red zone, making it difficult for opposing offenses to finish drives. Green Bay will need to find ways to take advantage of every opportunity it gets. Love's mobility could become an important part of that plan. He has the ability to extend plays when the pocket breaks down, and the Packers could use him as a runner when they get close to the goal line.

Whether Josh Jacobs is available or not, Love will have opportunities to use his legs. If Jacobs misses the game, Green Bay could lean even more heavily on Love's legs to help finish drives.

Jordan Love could have a difficult time getting comfortable in the pocket on Wednesday night. Myles Garrett and Aaron Donald are two of the best defensive players in the NFL, and both will have opportunities to pressure the Packers quarterback.

The Packers may still put together a productive offensive performance, but these two defensive stars will make their presence felt. Whether they each record two sacks or one of them gets three while the other adds one, don't be shocked if they combine for at least four.

Both teams had extra time to prepare for this matchup, which should help their defensive fronts come out ready to attack. Garrett has three sacks in three games this season, while Donald has 1.5 sacks in eight appearances.

The numbers have not been there yet, but Wednesday night could be the game where they put together their best performance of the season. Love will have to find ways to get the ball out quickly and avoid holding onto it for too long, or use his legs as I mentioned above.

This could be the toughest prediction to make, but it could also be the easiest if Parsons is cleared to play.

The Packers have been waiting to get their star pass rusher back since he tore his ACL last season. His arrival in Green Bay was one of the biggest moves of the offseason, and his presence helped change the outlook for the defense.

Parsons was dominant during his time with the Dallas Cowboys, consistently creating pressure and making life difficult for opposing quarterbacks. The Packers were counting on him to help take their defense to another level before his injury interrupted those plans.

Now, Parsons appears to be nearing his return, with Wednesday night's game against the Rams potentially marking his 2026 debut. He still needs final medical clearance, but he has been practicing and appears to be in position to play.

If Parsons is on the field, I expect him to make an immediate impact against Matthew Stafford. The Rams are missing both Puka Nacua and Davante Adams, leaving them without their top two receiving options. That could lead to more reliance on the running game, but Green Bay will still have opportunities to pressure Stafford.

Two sacks might be asking a lot in Parsons' first game back, especially considering he could be on a snap count. However, he is one of the few players capable of taking over a game defensively, and the Packers need him to make an impact as they push toward the postseason.

If Parsons plays Wednesday night, he will sack Stafford twice and remind everyone what Green Bay has been missing.
